B.3.3。误差传播分析B.3.3. ERROR PROPAGATION ANALYSIS

上述分析通过跨模型一致性来验证每一步。我们进一步分析残余上游错误是否传播到下游裁决错误。我们的人工验证不是一组孤立的模块验证，而是对最终基准测试正确性的端到端验证。为了更好地理解错误传播，我们将管道分解为两个正确性路径：（1）审查分类The above analysis validates each step through cross-model consistency. We further analyze whether residual upstream errors propagate to downstream adjudication errors. Our human verification is not a set of isolated module validations, but an end-to-end validation of the final benchmark’s correctness. To better understand error propagation, we decompose the pipeline into two correctness paths: (1) review classification→审稿人与作者冲突裁决（Clsreviewer-author conflict adjudication (Cls→RA），以及（2）审查分类RA), and (2) review classification→意见分组opinion grouping→审稿人-审稿人冲突裁决（Clsreviewer-reviewer conflict adjudication (Cls→组Grp→RR）。对于每条路径，我们比较干净和不完美的上游条件下的下游裁决误差。RR). For each path, we compare downstream adjudication errors under clean and imperfect upstream conditions.

首先，上游结构化步骤相对可靠。在 50 篇人工注释的论文上，评论分类的准确率达到 85.45%，意见分组的准确率达到 93.41%。其次，残余上游错误确实会传播，但它们并不是下游错误的主要来源。如表10所示，在审稿人-作者路径中，加权下游裁决错误率从干净上游条件下的31.41%增加到不完善条件下的47.45%First, the upstream structured steps are relatively reliable. On 50 human-annotated papers, review classification achieves 85.45% accuracy, and opinion grouping achieves 93.41% accuracy. Second, residual upstream errors do propagate, but they are not the dominant source of downstream errors. As shown in Table 10, in the reviewer-author path, the weighted downstream adjudication error rate increases from 31.41% under clean upstream conditions to 47.45% under imperfect

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

上游条件。这导致传播损失为 16.04 个百分点。在审阅者-审阅者路径中，不完美上游子集仅包含两个条目，因此我们不报告单独的加权不完美上游错误率。然而，在两条路径中，大多数下游错误词仍然来自具有干净上游输出的条目：上游不完美条目分别仅占两条路径中下游错误词的15.73%和3.17%。upstream conditions. This gives a propagation penalty of 16.04 percentage points. In the reviewer-reviewer path, the imperfect-upstream subset contains only two entries, so we do not report a separate weighted imperfect-upstream error rate. Nevertheless, in both paths, most downstream error words still come from entries with clean upstream outputs: upstream-imperfect entries account for only 15.73% and 3.17% of downstream error words in the two paths, respectively.

第三，下游错误在论文中的分布并不均匀。将论文层面的评审条目汇总后，平均正确率为 66.67%，但分布极不均匀：48.00% 的论文完全正确，18.00% 完全错误，只有 12.00% 处于 40-60% 正确率范围内。这表明下游错误集中在困难论文的子集中，而不是均匀地影响所有论文。因此，传播惩罚不应被解释为仅由步级错误传输引起。部分残余误差也反映了任务的内在难度，因为评论和讨论上下文越模糊的论文就越难裁决。Third, downstream errors are not uniformly distributed across papers. After aggregating adjudication entries at the paper level, the average correctness ratio is 66.67%, but the distribution is highly uneven: 48.00% of papers are entirely correct, 18.00% are entirely incorrect, and only 12.00% fall into the middle 40–60% correctness range. This suggests that downstream errors are concentrated in a subset of difficult papers rather than evenly affecting all papers. Therefore, the propagation penalty should not be interpreted as arising solely from step-level error transmission. Part of the residual error also reflects intrinsic task difficulty, since papers with more ambiguous comments and discussion contexts are harder to adjudicate.

总体而言，残余误差确实沿着两条正确性路径传播，但效果有限。鉴于上游结构化步骤的准确度相对较高，并且上游问题导致下游错误的一小部分，错误传播不是最终基准错误的唯一来源，也不是主要来源。Overall, residual errors do propagate along both correctness paths, but the effect is limited. Given the relatively high accuracy of the upstream structured steps and the small fraction of downstream errors attributable to upstream issues, error propagation is not the only source, nor the dominant source, of final benchmark errors.

To verify that this subsampling does not materially change the aggregate scoring behavior, we run experiments on the full set of 3,900 papers with four representative models and the human leave-one-out score, and compare the model-level dimension averages computed on the 1,300-sample subset and on the full 3,900 set in Table 11. CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

B.5.为AI审稿人评估提供更好的参考B.5. Better Reference for AI Reviewer Evaluation

人类同行评审通常不完整或不正确，这可能导致人工智能评审员的表现不佳。因此，在本附录中，我们探索了一个单独的综合设置，旨在为基准评审系统生成更强的参考；这与主要的基准构建管道不同，后者仅注释和过滤现有的人工评论。具体来说，在正文中，我们只评估人类和人工智能评论都可用的类别，这可能导致覆盖不完整。因此，我们探索数据合成，以构建超越现有人类评论限制的更全面、更可靠的参考。Human peer reviews are often incomplete or incorrect, which can lead to suboptimal AI reviewers. In this appendix, we therefore explore a separate synthesis setting that aims to generate stronger references for benchmarking reviewer systems; this is distinct from the main benchmark-construction pipeline, which only annotates and filters existing human reviews. Specifically, in the main text, we only evaluate categories where both human and AI comments are available, which can lead to incomplete coverage. We therefore explore data synthesis to construct more comprehensive and reliable references beyond the limitations of existing human reviews.

完整性感知代理工作流程。Completeness-Aware Agent Workflow.为了解决人类评论的类别覆盖不完整的问题，我们训练分类代理来解决这个问题，类似于我们在正文中使用分类子基准的方式。每个代理都会针对单个子类别生成评论，我们将所有代理的输出连接到最终评论中，这自然确保了全面的类别覆盖。如果某个子类别缺乏对论文的人工评论，我们就会跳过该代理的训练，以避免学习缺失类别的偏差。我们使用 CoCoReviewBench 中未包含的 ICLR 2017-2025 和 NeurIPS 2021-2024 论文，并过滤掉包含超过 23k token 的论文，产生 34k 篇论文和 130k 评论意见。然后，我们使用 ReviewSplit 和 ReviewClassify 将它们拆分并分类为子类别级别的评论。我们将过短的类别注释视为缺失，并忽略与图形相关的类别，从而产生 321k 个类别-论文对用于训练。To tackle the incomplete category coverage of human reviews, we train categorical agents to address this issue, similar to how we use categorical sub-benchmarks in the main text. Each agent generates comments for a single subcategory, and we concatenate all agents’ outputs into the final review, which naturally ensures comprehensive category coverage. If a subcategory lacks human comments for a paper, we skip training that agent to avoid learning missing-category biases. We use papers from ICLR 2017–2025 and NeurIPS 2021–2024 that are not included in CoCoReviewBench, and filter out papers containing over 23k tokens, yielding 34k papers and 130k review comments. We then split and classify them into subcategory-level comments using ReviewSplit and ReviewClassify. We treat overly short category comments as missing and ignore Figure-related categories, resulting in 321k category–paper pairs for training.

正确性意识训练数据构建。Correctness-Aware Training Data Construction.直接连接同一类别的不同审稿人的评论可能会导致风格/格式不一致、冗余和错误，而运行完整的 CoCoReviewBench 过滤管道成本太高。因此，我们训练 ReviewClean 使用 CoCoReviewBench 构建过程中的中间输出来删除重复数据并删除不正确的意见。这项任务相对困难，因此我们的目标是使模型能够将多个评论的意见合并为流畅的文本，同时尽可能删除重复和不正确的意见，而不追求过高的准确性。Directly concatenating different reviewers’ comments of the same category can introduce stylistic/formatting inconsistencies, redundancy, and errors, while running the full CoCoReviewBench filtering pipeline is too costly. We therefore train ReviewClean to deduplicate and remove incorrect opinions using intermediate outputs from the CoCoReviewBench construction process. This task is relatively difficult, so our goal is to enable the model to merge opinions from multiple reviews into fluent text, while deleting repeated and incorrect opinions as much as possible, without pursuing overly high accuracy.

为了构建 ReviewClean 训练数据，我们使用 Qwen3-8B 将每个类别中经过清理的、非重复的、非冲突的评论重写为流畅的文本。然后，我们将重复数据删除/冲突删除之前的噪声版本与重写的清理版本作为训练数据进行配对，以便 ReviewClean 学习删除重复/冲突的意见并修复格式。我们遵循 ReviewClassify 训练设置，但训练两个 epoch，批量大小为 128。最后，我们应用 ReviewClean 生成类别级注释，以调整代理工作流程的指令。To build ReviewClean training data, we use Qwen3-8B to rewrite the cleaned, non-duplicated, and non-conflicting reviews in each category into fluent text. We then pair the noisy version before deduplication/conflict removal with this rewritten cleaned version as training data, so that ReviewClean learns to remove duplicated/conflicting opinions and fix formatting. We follow the ReviewClassify training setup, but train for two epochs with a batch size of 128. Finally, we apply ReviewClean to produce category-level comments for instruction tuning of our agent workflow.

实验设置。Experimental Setup.我们使用类似于 ReviewClassify 的指令调整设置来训练该模型，从而生成 ReviewAgent 模型。我们还考虑一个简单的基线，将所有人类审稿人评论连接起来作为参考，并训练模型以生成尽可能全面的评论，称为 ReviewMerge。在表12中，我们还报告了ReviewSingle和几个强大的单模型AI审稿人以供参考。在所有实验中，我们使用相同的设置：批量大小 256、最多 1,200 个步骤、最大序列长度 32k，其他设置与 ReviewClassify 一致。在推理时，我们使用非推理模式，超参数与主要实验保持一致。We train this model using an instruction-tuning setup similar to ReviewClassify, yielding the ReviewAgent model. We also consider a simple baseline that concatenates all human reviewer comments as references and trains a model to generate as comprehensive comments as possible, referred to as ReviewMerge. In Table 12, we additionally report ReviewSingle and several strong single-model AI reviewers for reference. Across all experiments, we use the same settings: batch size 256, a maximum of 1,200 steps, and a maximum sequence length of 32k, with other settings consistent with ReviewClassify. At inference time, we use the non-reasoning mode, with hyperparameters kept consistent with the main experiments.

我们的模型生成的改进参考。Improved References Generated by Our Model.结果如表 12 所示。虽然 GPT-5.2 的平均水平仍然稍强，但 ReviewAgent 在大多数指标上都远远优于更简单的 ReviewMerge 和 ReviewSingle 基线，并且在大多数指标上都超过了人类参考和强大的单模型基线。我们的方法进行 22 次模型调用来生成特定于类别的评论，从而产生更多内容。然而，从构建更可靠参考的角度来看，这些结果表明，结构化多阶段合成可以比直接基于合并的基线得到显着改进，同时仍然为未来在风格质量和效率方面的工作留有空间。为了可靠性和出处，在正文中，我们仍然使用人工评论作为参考，这通常被认为更值得信赖。The results are shown in Table 12. ReviewAgent substantially outperforms the simpler ReviewMerge and ReviewSingle baselines across most metrics, and also exceeds both human references and strong single-model baselines across most metrics, although GPT-5.2 still remains slightly stronger on average. Our approach makes 22 model calls to generate category-specific comments and therefore produces more content. Nevertheless, from the perspective of constructing more reliable references, these results suggest that structured multi-stage synthesis can improve substantially over direct merge-based baselines, while still leaving room for future work on stylistic quality and efficiency. For reliability and provenance, in the main text, we still use human reviews as references, which are often considered more trustworthy.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

B.6.分类得分分析B.6. Categorical Score Analysis

我们的基准包括多个评审类别和多个评估维度，可以实现多视角评估和细粒度分析。在正文中，我们已经提到，AI Reviewer 在质量相关类别上表现良好，保持了较高的正确性和分析深度。在这里，我们进一步报告其他维度的分数并进行更细粒度的分析。完整结果如图 7 所示。Our benchmark includes multiple review categories and multiple evaluation dimensions, enabling multi-perspective evaluation and fine-grained analysis. In the main text, we have already mentioned that the AI Reviewer performs well on Quality-related categories, maintaining high correctness and analytical depth. Here, we further report scores along additional dimensions and conduct a finer-grained analysis. The full results are shown in Figure 7.

图 7.Figure 7.CoCoReviewBench 中主要评论类别的分类指标分数。面板（a-f）对应于正确性、彻底性、基础性、可验证性、清晰度和平均分数。 x 轴列出了主要评论类别。在每个面板中，类别按人工智能与人类的分数差距排序。颜色表示相应的主要审查类别，蓝色对应质量，橙色对应清晰度，绿色对应原创性，红色对应重要性，紫色对应政策。Categorical metric scores across major review categories in CoCoReviewBench. Panels (a–f) correspond to correctness, thoroughness, grounding, verifiability, clarity, and the average score. The x-axis lists the major review categories. Within each panel, categories are ordered by the AI–Human score gap. Colors indicate the corresponding major review categories, with blue corresponding to Quality, orange to Clarity, green to Originality, red to Significance, and purple to Policy.

清晰度仍然是人工智能审稿人最一贯的弱点。Clarity remains the most consistent weakness for AI reviewers.在主要评论类别中，清晰度类别显示出人工智能评论者最一致的负面差距。它在正确性、基础性、可验证性，尤其是最终平均分方面排名接近较弱的一端。这表明，尽管人工智能审稿本身通常很流畅，但目前的人工智能审稿人在判断论文是否写得清楚、组织良好和解释充分方面仍然不太有效。Across major review categories, the Clarity category shows the most consistent negative gap for AI reviewers. It ranks near the weaker end in correctness, grounding, verifiability, and especially in the final average score. This indicates that, although AI reviews themselves are usually fluent, current AI reviewers are still less effective at judging whether the paper is clearly written, well organized, and sufficiently explained.

人工智能政策审查的正确性和彻底性较弱，而人类政策审查的形式维度较弱。AI policy reviews are weak in correctness and thoroughness, while human policy reviews are weak in formal dimensions.政策类别表现出独特的模式。在正确性和彻底性方面，人工智能评审员的表现比人类差，这表明与政策相关的判断仍然不可靠且不够深入。然而，对于更正式的维度（例如基础性、可验证性和清晰度），该模式有所不同。在这些维度上，人类的政策评论本身就相对较弱，而人工智能评论者可以获得相当甚至更好的相对分数。一个可能的原因是，人类审阅者经常简短地、程序性地提出政策问题，而人工智能审阅者往往会提供更明确的解释。因此，人工智能审稿人在政策方面相对较高的正式分数不应被解释为更强的政策判断，因为正确性和彻底性的核心维度仍然较弱。The Policy category exhibits a distinct pattern. In correctness and thoroughness, AI reviewers perform worse than humans, showing that policy-related judgments remain unreliable and insufficiently deep. However, the pattern is different for more formal dimensions such as grounding, verifiability, and clarity. In these dimensions, human Policy comments are themselves relatively weak, and AI reviewers can obtain comparable or even better relative scores. One possible reason is that human reviewers often raise policy concerns briefly and procedurally, whereas AI reviewers tend to provide more explicit explanations. Therefore, the relatively better formal scores of AI reviewers in Policy should not be interpreted as stronger policy judgment, since the core dimensions of correctness and thoroughness remain weak.

人工智能审阅者可以在质量的基础和可验证性方面与人类相媲美。AI reviewers can match humans in grounding and verifiability for Quality.正如正文中所讨论的，就正确性和彻底性而言，质量对于人工智能审稿人来说已经是相对较强的类别之一。在这里，我们进一步发现这种优势也延伸到了证据相关的维度。对于质量类别，人工审核员表现出强大的基础和可验证性。这是预料之中的，因为与质量相关的评论通常涉及具体的技术问题。此类评论往往在论文中有更清晰的证据，也更容易验证。重要的是，人工智能审阅者在这一类别的基础性和可验证性方面可以在很大程度上与人类相匹配，这表明当前的人工智能审阅者可以提供具有可比较的特异性和可检查性的反馈。As discussed in the main text, Quality is already one of the relatively stronger categories for AI reviewers in terms of correctness and thoroughness. Here, we further find that this advantage also extends to the evidence-related dimensions. For the Quality category, human reviewers show strong grounding and verifiability. This is expected because Quality-related comments usually concern concrete technical issues. Such comments often have clearer evidence in the paper and are easier to verify. Importantly, AI reviewers can largely match humans on grounding and verifiability in this category, suggesting that current AI reviewers can provide feedback with comparable specificity and checkability.

原创性和意义保持较强的表现。Originality and Significance maintain relatively strong performance.原创性和重要性在评估维度上保持相对良好的表现。它们没有表现出政策中观察到的严重正确性和彻底性弱点，也没有表现出清晰度中观察到的广泛退化。在平均分面板中，原创性和重要性在五个主要类别中达到了人工智能与人类差距的前两位，这表明当前的人工智能审稿人在高水平贡献和影响评估方面比在政策合规性或详细清晰度诊断方面相对更强。Originality and Significance maintain relatively favorable performance across the evaluation dimensions. They do not show the severe correctness and thoroughness weakness observed in Policy, nor the broad degradation observed in Clarity. In the average-score panel, Originality and Significance achieve the top two AI–Human gaps among the five major categories, indicating that current AI reviewers are comparatively stronger at high-level contribution and impact assessment than at policy compliance or detailed clarity diagnosis.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

B.7.人工智能审稿人的幻觉B.7. Hallucination in AI Reviewers

图中AI审稿幻觉案例研究。Case Study of AI Reviewer Hallucination in Figure.尽管我们的评估使用纯文本输入，即模型无法访问图形，但我们偶尔会在人工智能评论中观察到特定于图形的评论。我们将这种现象称为与图形相关的幻觉：模型产生自信、具体的批评，隐含地假设输入中不可用的视觉证据。虽然这样的观点总体上很少见，但它们令人担忧，因为它们模仿了看似合理的同行评审反馈，并且在没有出处的情况下可能会误导作者和读者。Although our evaluation uses text-only inputs, i.e., models do not have access to figures, we occasionally observe figure-specific comments in AI reviews. We refer to this phenomenon as figure-related hallucination: the model produces confident, concrete critiques that implicitly assume visual evidence that is not available in the input. While such opinions are rare in aggregate, they are concerning because they mimic plausible peer review feedback and can mislead authors and readers when presented without provenance.

图 8 显示了三个代表性案例。左：Gemini-3-Pro 批评标题中提到“z 轴”，并质疑与“2D”图的一致性，尽管没有看到该图。中：Qwen3-32B 断言“图 2/3”中的定性分析缺乏多样化的示例，在无法访问底层视觉效果的情况下构建了视觉缺陷。右图：DeepReview14B 声称“图 8”的记录很少，并且缺乏清晰的轴标签和缩放信息。总的来说，这些案例表明，过滤不充分的训练数据可能会导致模型产生不匹配但有说服力的批评。Figure 8 shows three representative cases. Left: Gemini-3-Pro criticizes the caption for mentioning a “z-axis” and questions consistency with “2D” plots, despite not seeing the figure. Middle: Qwen3-32B asserts that the qualitative analysis in “Figure 2/3” lacks diverse examples, framing a visual deficiency without access to the underlying visuals. Right: DeepReview14B claims that “Figure 8” is poorly documented and lacks clear axis labels and scaling information. Collectively, these cases suggest that insufficiently filtered training data can lead models to generate mismatched yet persuasive critiques.

图 8.Figure 8.人工智能评论产生幻觉的案例。在每种情况下，提供适当的审查反馈都需要了解数字。然而，这些例子中的人工智能审阅者都没有得到数字作为输入。Cases of hallucinated AI reviews. In each case, providing appropriate review feedback requires understanding the figure. However, none of the AI reviewers in these examples are given the figure as input.

与错误观点的相似度更高。Higher Similarity to Incorrect Opinions.此外，我们还研究了更广泛的幻觉现象是否存在于更广泛的类别中。 CoCoReviewBench 将意见标记为正确或不正确，并构建一组不正确的意见。与这些错误观点的较高相似度可以被视为幻觉，即错误地拟合数据中的错误观点。具体来说，与正文相比，我们只使用正确的意见作为黄金参考（w/ Correct），这里我们只使用不正确的意见作为参考（w/ In Correct）并报告最终的正确性得分，它代表了人工智能评论与相应参考文献之间的相似度。为了减少干扰，我们忽略清晰度图/表格子类别，因为我们在评估期间不输入图像。Further, we examine whether broader hallucination phenomena exist across a wider range of categories. CoCoReviewBench labels opinions as correct or incorrect and constructs a set of incorrect opinions. Higher similarity to these incorrect opinions can be regarded as hallucination, i.e., incorrectly fitting the erroneous opinions in the data. Specifically, compared with the main text, where we only use correct opinions as the gold reference (w/ Correct), here we use only incorrect opinions as the reference (w/ Incorrect) and report the final Correctness score, which represents the similarity between the AI review and the corresponding reference. To reduce interference, we ignore the Clarity-Figure/Table subcategory since we do not input images during evaluation.

结果如表 13 所示。人类对错误意见的认同度下降，证明了错误意见提取的有效性。在人工智能方面，所有模型的模式并不统一，但包括 GPT-5.2、GPT-5-Mini、Gemini-3-Pro/Flash 和 Qwen3-32B 在内的几个强大的系统显示出比人类更高的错误意见相似度，平均行也略高于人类基线。这表明如果使用不正确的意见作为训练或评估的参考，则存在可测量的幻觉风险（Deng et al., 2023; Liu et al., 2024b; Ke et al., 2025），即使一些较弱的模型与这些不正确的参考仍然不太一致。The results are shown in Table 13. Humans’ agreement with incorrect opinions decreases, demonstrating the validity of incorrect-opinion extraction. On the AI side, the pattern is not uniform across all models, but several strong systems, including GPT-5.2, GPT-5-Mini, Gemini-3-Pro/Flash, and Qwen3-32B, show higher similarity to incorrect opinions than humans, and the average row is also slightly above the human baseline. This indicates a measurable hallucination risk if incorrect opinions are used as references for training or evaluation (Deng et al., 2023; Liu et al., 2024b; Ke et al., 2025), even though some weaker models remain less aligned with these incorrect references.

C. 案例研究C. Case Study

C.1.审稿人与作者冲突的原因C.1. Causes of Reviewer-Author Conflicts

为了进一步分析审稿人与作者冲突的原因，我们随机选择了 50 个人工标记的冲突实例，发现它们可以分为三种类型：误解、忽视细节和先前评估不准确。我们提出三个案例来说明这些不同类型的差异如何在同行评审过程中体现。To further analyze the causes of reviewer–author conflicts, we randomly select 50 human-labeled conflict instances and find that they can be categorized into three types: misunderstandings, overlooked details, and inaccurate prior assessments. We present three cases to illustrate how these different types of discrepancies manifest in the peer review process.

案例一：误解。Case 1: Misunderstanding.误解被定义为审稿人误解论文的主要贡献、逻辑或目标的情况。如图 9（左）所示，审稿人将论文的贡献误解为对现有组件的增量重用。在反驳中，作者阐明了该研究的核心定位，并解释了为什么这种还原论的描述未能抓住关键的技术创新。至关重要的是，元审查承认了该研究的贡献并支持作者的澄清。这个案例表明，冲突是由解释不一致引起的，而不是研究本身的实质性缺陷。Misunderstanding is defined as a scenario where the reviewer misunderstands the main contribution, logic, or goal of the paper. As shown in Figure 9 (Left), the reviewer misinterprets the paper’s contribution as an incremental reuse of existing components. In the rebuttal, the authors clarify the core positioning of the research and explain why such a reductionist characterization fails to capture the key technical innovation. Crucially, the meta-review acknowledges the contribution of the study and supports the authors’ clarification. This case demonstrates a conflict driven by interpretive misalignment rather than a substantive flaw in the research itself.

案例2：遗漏细节。Case 2: Missed Details.遗漏细节被定义为审稿人遗漏了论文中明确存在的特定技术细节、公式、参数设置或实验结果的情况。图 9（中）说明了这种情况，审稿人提出的问题和要求的信息实际上存在于原始提交中。作者随后指出了相关内容的具体位置。这个案例凸显了审稿人对细节的疏忽而产生的冲突，导致了多余的批评。Missed details is defined as instances where the reviewer misses specific technical details, formulas, parameter settings, or experimental results that are explicitly present in the paper. Figure 9 (Middle) illustrates this scenario, where the questions raised and information requested by the reviewer actually exist in the original submission. The authors subsequently point out the specific locations of the relevant content. This case highlights a conflict resulting from the reviewer’s oversight of details, leading to redundant critiques.

案例3：事前评估不准确。Case 3: Inaccurate Prior Assessment.不准确的事先评估被定义为审稿人基于主观偏见、未经证实的前提或标准做法批评作品的情况，导致作者在不正确的假设下做出回应。与前两种类型表明审稿人没有仔细阅读论文不同，这种类型反映了审稿人之前的幻觉评估，导致了错误的审稿。如图9（右）所示，审稿人自信地声称这些定理遵循特定先前作品的“标准技术”，质疑论文的新颖性。在反驳中，作者反驳了这一说法，指出这些具体结果并未出现在现有文献中。元审查承认该方法的新颖性和实际影响。这个案例说明了当审稿人毫无根据的外部假设导致对新颖性的评估不准确时，就会产生冲突。Inaccurate prior assessment is defined as a situation where the reviewer criticizes the work based on subjective bias, unsubstantiated premises, or standard practices, leading the authors to respond under incorrect assumptions. Unlike the previous two types, which indicate that the reviewer does not read the paper carefully, this type reflects a reviewer’s hallucinated prior assessment, resulting in an erroneous review. As shown in Figure 9 (Right), the reviewer confidently claims that the theorems follow “standard techniques” from specific prior works, questioning the paper’s novelty. In the rebuttal, the authors refute this claim, noting that these specific results do not appear in the existing literature. The meta-review acknowledges the novelty and practical impact of the method. This case illustrates a conflict that arises when a reviewer’s baseless external assumption leads to an inaccurate assessment of novelty.

C.2. CoCoReviewBench可靠性分析C.2. Analysis of CoCoReviewBench Reliability

对于审稿人和作者冲突引起的错误检测，我们在人工验证中实现了 66.83% 的准确率。这种准确性明显低于其他注释步骤，并且被拒绝和接受的论文之间存在巨大差异：50.03% 与 79.76%。为了调查这种差异的根本原因，我们对分歧更为明显的被拒绝的论文进行了具体案例分析。For error detection caused by Reviewer-Author conflicts, we achieve 66.83% accuracy in human verification. This accuracy is notably lower than other annotation steps, and exhibits a substantial disparity between rejected and accepted papers: 50.03% versus 79.76%. To investigate the underlying causes of this discrepancy, we conduct case-specific analyses on rejected papers where disagreement is more pronounced.

我们分析被拒绝的论文中的项目，其中人工注释者将我们数据集中的审稿人与作者冲突分类为不正确。如图10所示，我们提出了两个具有代表性的案例，其中作者提供了基于事实的反驳，有效地反驳了审稿人的技术误解。至关重要的是，相应的元审查未能裁决这些具体争议，而是引用了支持拒绝的一般理由。受到元评论对论文消极态度的影响，人类注释者将我们的数据集标记为错误，这意味着评论者的批评不包含错误。这揭示了当元审查总体负面时，人类注释者倾向于与批判性评估保持一致，导致他们忽视客观有效的反驳，特别是当元审查缺乏对特定争议点的详细论证时。We analyze items in rejected papers where human annotators classify the Reviewer-Author conflicts in our dataset as incorrect. As shown in Figure 10, we present two representative cases in which the authors provide factually grounded rebuttals that effectively refute the reviewers’ technical misconceptions. Crucially, the corresponding meta-review fails to adjudicate these specific disputes, instead citing general reasons to support the rejection. Influenced by the negative attitude of the meta-review toward the paper, human annotators label our dataset as erroneous, implying that the reviewers’ critiques contained no errors. This reveals a tendency for human annotators to align with critical assessments when the meta-review is overall negative, leading them to overlook objectively valid rebuttals, particularly when the meta-review lacks detailed justification for the specific contested point.

这些观察结果表明，较低的准确率主要归因于偏见，负面裁决会追溯影响注释者对反驳有效性的判断。这表明这一步的注释准确性实际上更高，证明了我们方法的有效性和任务的固有难度。These observations suggest that the lower accuracy rate is primarily attributable to bias, whereby the negative verdict retroactively influences annotators’ judgment of the rebuttal’s validity. This indicates that the annotation accuracy for this step is actually higher, demonstrating both the effectiveness of our method and the inherent difficulty of the task.

D. 附加实验设置D. Additional Experiment Setup

人类参考。Human References.对于包括 BLEU、ROUGE-L 和 BERTScore 在内的旧指标，我们遵循Zhang 等人的方法。 （2025）计算人工智能评论和每个人类评论者的评论之间的相似度。在这里，与之前的工作一致，我们只考虑审稿人的第一次审稿作为参考。然而，进一步的答复也很有价值。因此，在我们的基准测试中，我们还添加了进一步的回复作为参考，在与作者讨论后可能包含更正确或更深入的意见。而且，在讨论同一主题的所有意见中，我们只保留最长的正确意见作为代表。如果一个小组中的所有意见都不正确，我们就会放弃该小组。这确保了引用是非冗余且正确的。对于我们论文级别的法学硕士法官评估，我们使用所有此类意见作为参考，而分类评估仅考虑分配给相应子类别的意见组。最后，为了确保与AI审稿人的公平比较，对于每个被评估的人类审稿人，我们只保留第一个审稿人，并以与AI审稿人相同的方式应用ReviewClassify模型进行分类。此外，对于类别完整性（Complete.）指标中的满分参考，即所有审稿人汇总的类别覆盖率，我们还使用ReviewClassify模型进行重新分类。在其他情况下，我们仍然使用 CoCoReviewBench 注释作为参考。此外，在上面的所有实验中，我们都使用纯文本输入。因此，在构建人类参考文献时，我们删除了需要数字的评论。For the old metrics including BLEU, ROUGE-L, and BERTScore, we follow Zhang et al. (2025) to compute the similarity between an AI review and each human reviewer’s comments. Here, consistent with prior work, we only consider the reviewer’s first review as references. However, further replies are also valuable. Therefore, in our benchmark, we additionally include further replies as references, which may contain more correct or deeper opinions after discussion with the authors. Moreover, among all opinions that discuss the same topic, we keep only the longest correct opinion as the representative. If all opinions in a group are incorrect, we drop the group. This ensures that the references are non-redundant and correct. For our paper-level LLM-as-a-judge evaluation, we use all such opinions as references, while categorical evaluation considers only the opinion groups assigned to the corresponding subcategory. Finally, to ensure a fair comparison with AI reviewers, for each evaluated human reviewer, we keep only the first review and apply the ReviewClassify model for classification in the same way as AI Reviewers. In addition, for the full-score reference in the category completeness (Complete.) metric, i.e., the category coverage aggregated from all reviewers, we also use the ReviewClassify model for re-classification. In other cases, we still use the CoCoReviewBench annotations for the references. Besides, in all the experiments above, we use text-only inputs. Accordingly, when constructing the human references, we remove reviews that require figures.

ReviewSplit 和 ReviewClassify 的训练详细信息。Training Details of ReviewSplit and ReviewClassify.ReviewSplit 使用没有 KL 损失的 GRPO 进行训练，直接训练模型的推理模式，温度为 0.7，最大输入长度和最大输出长度均设置为 12k 个 token（Deng 等人，2025b；Rao 等人，2025）。奖励在正文中定义。训练数据包括二次分割步骤后 CoCoReviewBench 的分割结果。在这里，我们使用 2 个 epoch 的全参数训练，每个问题生成 32 个轨迹，每批生成 128 个轨迹，学习率为 1e-6。 ReviewClassify 使用指令调优进行训练（Wei et al., 2022），训练其非推理模式，而测试则使用推理模式。它通过低秩自适应进行微调（LoRA；Hu et al., 2022），使用 2e-5 的学习率、16 的 LoRA 秩和 32 的 LoRA alpha，将 LoRA 应用于所有线性层。训练数据包括二次分割步骤前后 CoCoReviewBench 的意见分类结果，使模型能够学习如何处理具有不同数量原子意见的输入。我们训练一个 epoch，批量大小为 256。ReviewSplit is trained using GRPO without KL loss, directly training the model’s reasoning mode with a temperature of 0.7, and with both the maximum input length and maximum output length set to 12k tokens (Deng et al., 2025b; Rao et al., 2025). The reward is defined in the main text. The training data include the split results of CoCoReviewBench after the Secondary Split step. Here, we use full-parameter training for 2 epochs, generating 32 trajectories per question and 128 trajectories per batch, with a learning rate of 1e-6. ReviewClassify is trained using instruction tuning (Wei et al., 2022), training its non-reasoning mode, while testing uses the reasoning mode. It is fine-tuned with low-rank adaptation (LoRA; Hu et al., 2022), using a learning rate of 2e-5, a LoRA rank of 16, and a LoRA alpha of 32, applying LoRA to all linear layers. The training data include the opinion classification results of CoCoReviewBench before and after the Secondary Split step, enabling the model to learn how to handle inputs with different numbers of atomic opinions. We train for one epoch with a batch size of 256.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

E、提示E. Prompts

E.1。 CoCoreviewBench 构建提示E.1. Prompts for CoCoreviewBench Construction

我们在下表中列出了论文第 4 节中使用的所有提示，包括：We present all the prompts used in our paper in §4 in the following tables, including:

  • 评论细分提示：Prompts for Review Segmentation:提示用于在构建基准时将审稿评论和作者回复分割成原子意见。我们首先将审稿意见和作者回复按照规则分割成句子级单元，然后利用这个提示根据内容聚合句子级意见，从而产生多个原子意见和相应的作者回复。The prompt is used to split review comments and author responses into atomic opinions when constructing the benchmark. We first segment the review comments and author responses into sentencelevel units according to rules, then use this prompt to aggregate the sentence-level opinions based on content, resulting in multiple atomic opinions and corresponding author responses.

  • 评论分类提示：Prompts for Review Classification:该提示用于在构建基准时为上一步获得的原子意见分配类别标签。The prompt is used to assign category labels to the atomic opinions obtained from the previous step when constructing the benchmark.

  • 二次细分的提示：Prompts for Secondary Segmentation:该提示用于查看上面的标记结果。我们发现评论分割可能会将多个意见聚合到一个块中，从而导致带有过多标签的非原子意见。为了解决这个问题，对于具有多个标签的分段意见，我们尝试进一步拆分。具体来说，我们从可用的多个标签中为初步分割的意见中的每个句子重新分配最合适的标签。如果一个意见的不同部分属于不同的标签组，我们将其拆分为单独的意见，并根据每个意见的句子标签确定每个二次拆分意见的标签，从而解决这个问题。The prompt is used to review the labeling results above. We find that Review Segmentation may aggregate multiple opinions into one block, resulting in non-atomic opinions with excessive labels. To address this, for segmented opinions with multiple labels, we attempt further splitting. Specifically, we reassign the most suitable label to each sentence within the preliminarily segmented opinions from the available multiple labels. If different parts of an opinion belong to different label groups, we split it into separate opinions and determine the label of each secondary-split opinion based on its sentence labels, thereby resolving this issue.

  • 查看聚类的提示：Prompts for Review Clustering:该提示用于根据共享相同类别标签的原子意见是否讨论相同的特定内容（例如，多个原子意见都讨论同一公式的正确性）来进一步聚类。The prompt is used to further cluster atomic opinions that share the same category label based on whether they discuss the same specific content (e.g., several atomic opinions all discussing the correctness of the same formula).

  • 审稿人间冲突识别的提示：Prompts for Inter-Reviewer Conflict Identification:该提示用于识别由评论聚类得出的原子意见组内是否存在冲突，即识别评论者对所讨论的特定内容的看法是否一致。The prompt is used to identify whether conflicts exist within atomic opinion groups derived from Review Clustering, i.e., to identify whether reviewers’ views on the specific content under discussion are consistent.

  • 审稿人间冲突解决的提示：Prompts for Inter-Reviewer Conflict Resolution:提示用于判断Inter-Reviewer冲突识别得到的冲突原子意见组中哪些原子意见是正确的，哪些是错误的，并引入元评审来辅助这一判断。The prompt is used to determine which atomic opinions are correct and which are incorrect within the conflicting atomic opinion groups obtained from Inter-Reviewer Conflict Identification, and the meta-review is introduced to assist in this judgment.

  • 审阅者与作者冲突识别的提示：Prompts for Reviewer-Author Conflict Identification:该提示用于确定作者是否明确不同意审阅者的特定原子审阅。The prompt is used to identify whether the Author explicitly disagrees with a specific atomic review from the Reviewer.

  • 审稿人与作者冲突解决的提示：Prompts for Reviewer-Author Conflict Resolution:该提示用于当审稿人-作者冲突识别步骤识别出作者明确反对的原子评论时确定谁的意见是正确的，并引入元审阅来辅助此判断。The prompt is used to determine whose opinion is correct when the Reviewer-Author Conflict Identification step identifies atomic comments explicitly opposed by the author, and the meta-review is introduced to assist in this judgment.

  • ReviewSplit 模型的提示：Prompts for ReviewSplit Model:该提示的目的与“审稿分段提示”类似，但它仅分段审稿意见，不包括作者回复。The purpose of this prompt is similar to “Prompts for Review Segmentation”, but it only segments review comments, excluding author responses.

  • ReviewClassify 模型的提示：Prompts for ReviewClassify Model:该提示的目的与“审稿分段提示”类似，但它仅分段审稿意见，不包括作者回复。The purpose of this prompt is similar to “Prompts for Review Segmentation”, but it only segments review comments, excluding author responses.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

评论细分提示Prompts for Review Segmentation

您将获得同行评审讨论，分为You are given a peer-review discussion split into{编号num}部分，每个部分都带有“ID：parts, each prefixed with "ID:{{ id }}”。每个部分要么是审稿人评论，要么是作者回复。 # 目标 为每个部分分配一个数字标签，以便有关同一讨论点的所有部分共享相同的标签。 - 要点：审稿人提出的一个原子优势、弱点或问题。 - 标签从 1 开始（“标签 1”、“标签 2”...）。使用相同的编号来表示审阅者的观点以及针对该观点的所有回复。 - 仅对没有技术或内容价值的纯粹礼貌性文本（例如“谢谢”）或纯粹的论文摘要使用“标签 N/A”。 - 如果某个部分是引文（例如“[1] ...”）但明确支持或质疑已知观点，请使用该观点的标签。不要使用“标签不适用”。 # 程序 1. 识别审稿人评论中的原子点并为每个原子点创建一个新标签。 - 如果单个评论包含多个点，请分配单独的标签。 - 如果审阅者稍后提出新的要点，请给他们新的标签。". Each part is either a reviewer comment or an author reply. # GOAL Assign a numeric label to EVERY part so that all parts about the same discussion Point share the same label. - Point: one atomic strength, weakness, or question raised by a reviewer. - Labels start at 1 ("Label 1", "Label 2", ...). Use the same number for the reviewer’s Point and all replies addressing it. - Use "Label N/A" only for purely polite text with no technical or content value (e.g., "Thank you") or for pure paper summaries of the paper. - If a part is a citation (e.g., "[1] ...") but clearly supports or questions a known Point, use that Point’s label. Do not use "Label N/A". # PROCEDURE 1. Identify atomic Points in reviewer comments and create a new label for each. - If a single comment contains multiple Points, assign separate labels. - If a reviewer raises new Points later, give them new labels.

  1. 对于每个作者的回复，分配其响应点的标签。 - 如果一个回复涉及多个点，请列出以逗号分隔的所有标签（例如“标签 2, 5”）。 - “力量”通常不需要回复。重点关注对“弱点”和“问题”的匹配答复。For each author reply, assign the label(s) of the Point(s) it responds to. - If one reply addresses multiple Points, list all labels separated by commas (e.g., "Label 2, 5"). - ’Strength’ typically does not require a reply. Focus on matching replies to ’weaknesses’ and ’questions’.

输出格式 精确生产OUTPUT FORMAT Produce exactly{编号num}行，每个部分一根，按顺序：第 k 部分：标签 X，或者，如果有多个：第 k 部分：标签 X、Ylines, one per part, in order: Part k: Label X or, if multiple: Part k: Label X, Y{分段审稿人评论和作者回复Segmented reviewer comments and author responses}

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审查分类提示（第 1 部分）Prompts for Review Classification (Part 1)

您会收到审稿人的评论（以及可选的作者的回复）。您的任务是使用以下分类法对评论进行分类。输出应仅包含子类别名称。You are given a reviewer comment (and optionally the authors’ response). Your task is to classify the comment using the following taxonomy. The output should only contain the sub-category name.

{分段单项审核意见Segmented Individual Item Review Comments}# 分类法# Taxonomy

  1. QUAL（质量）：提交的内容在技术上是否合理？主张是否得到充分支持（例如，通过理论分析或实验结果）？使用的方法是否合适？这是一项完整的工作还是正在进行中的工作？作者是否认真、诚实地评估其作品的优点和缺点？QUAL (Quality): Is the submission technically sound? Are claims well supported (e.g., by theoretical analysis or experimental results)? Are the methods used appropriate? Is this a complete piece of work or work in progress? Are the authors careful and honest about evaluating both the strengths and weaknesses of their work?

  2. QUAL-MET（方法论健全性）：评估所提出的算法、模型或系统架构在技术上是否正确，并且不存在概念或实现错误。QUAL-MET (Methodological Soundness): Evaluates whether the proposed algorithms, models or system architectures are technically correct and free of conceptual or implementation errors.

  3. 优点：强调该方法表述良好、数学上一致且实施正确；没有明显的重大缺陷。 - 弱点：指出概念错误、错误指定的目标、算法缺陷或优化方法的误用。Strength: Highlights that the method is well-formulated, mathematically consistent and correctly implemented; no major flaws are apparent. - Weakness: Points out conceptual errors, mis-specified objectives, algorithmic flaws or misuses of optimisation methods.

  4. QUAL-EXP（实验设计和评估）：评估实验设置的充分性：数据集、基线、评估指标、消融研究和统计测试的选择。QUAL-EXP (Experimental Design & Evaluation): Assesses the adequacy of the experimental setup: choice of datasets, baselines, evaluation metrics, ablation studies, and statistical tests.

  5. 优点：注意全面的实验、适当的基线、足够大的数据和适当的指标；实验令人信服地验证了主张。Strength: Notes comprehensive experiments, appropriate baselines, sufficiently large data and proper metrics; experiments convincingly validate claims.

  6. 弱点：指出基线不足、缺少标准基准、指标不适当或缺乏消融研究。Weakness: Points out inadequate baselines, missing standard benchmarks, inappropriate metrics or lack of ablation studies.

  7. QUAL-REP（再现性和实施细节）：考虑提交是否提供了足够的细节（例如，超参数、代码可用性、数据集分割）来复制工作以及实施是否遵循最佳实践。QUAL-REP (Reproducibility & Implementation Details): Considers whether the submission provides enough detail (e.g., hyperparameters, code availability, dataset splits) to replicate the work and whether the implementation follows best practices.

  8. 优点：推荐开源代码、详细的超参数、清晰的数据集描述和全面的训练细节，可轻松复制。Strength: Commends open-sourced code, detailed hyperparameters, clear dataset descriptions and comprehensive training details enabling easy reproduction.

  9. 弱点：缺乏代码、不完整的超参数、未指定的数据分割或其他阻碍复制的细节缺失。 - QUAL-CMP（与先前工作的比较）：评估论文是否与相关先前工作和最先进的方法进行充分比较。Weakness: Notes lack of code, incomplete hyperparameters, unspecified data splits or other missing details that hinder reproduction. - QUAL-CMP (Comparisons to Prior Work): Evaluates whether the paper sufficiently compares against relevant prior work and state-of-the-art methods.

  10. 优点：承认与最近和适当的基线进行彻底比较以及对相关工作的正确引用。Strength: Acknowledges thorough comparisons against recent and appropriate baselines and proper citation of related work.

  11. 弱点：缺少与众所周知的最新方法的比较、过时的基线或不完整的文献综述。Weakness: Notes missing comparisons to well-known recent methods, outdated baselines, or incomplete literature review.

  12. QUAL-STA（统计严谨性和验证）：评估是否正确应用统计分析（例如显着性检验、置信区间）以及报告的改进是否具有统计意义。QUAL-STA (Statistical Rigor & Validation): Evaluates whether statistical analyses (e.g., significance tests, confidence intervals) are properly applied and whether reported improvements are statistically meaningful.

  13. 优点：赞扬适当的统计测试、稳健的验证、置信区间和透明的方差报告。Strength: Praises appropriate statistical tests, robust validation, confidence intervals and transparent reporting of variance.

  14. 弱点：批评缺乏统计测试、依赖单次运行或滥用统计方法。Weakness: Criticises absence of statistical tests, reliance on single runs, or misuse of statistical methods.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审查分类提示（第 2 部分）Prompts for Review Classification (Part 2)

  1. CLAR（清晰度）：提交的内容写得清楚吗？组织得好吗？ （如果没有，请提出建设性建议以提高其清晰度。）它是否充分告知读者？ （请注意，一篇写得很好的论文为专家读者提供了足够的信息来重现其结果。）CLAR (Clarity): Is the submission clearly written? Is it well organized? (If not, please make constructive suggestions for improving its clarity.) Does it adequately inform the reader? (Note that a superbly written paper provides enough information for an expert reader to reproduce its results.)

  2. CLAR-WRT（写作、术语和算法演示）：涵盖整体写作质量和组织、术语和关键概念的精确性以及算法演示的清晰度（代码片段、伪代码、工作流程图）。CLAR-WRT (Writing, Terminology & Algorithm Presentation): Covers overall writing quality and organization, precision of terminology and key concepts, and the clarity of algorithm presentations (code snippets, pseudocode, workflow diagrams).

  3. 优点：章节结构良好，叙述流畅；简洁易读的散文；所有专业术语和假设均得到明确定义并一致使用；伪代码/代码/工作流程是独立的，文档齐全（清晰的变量名称，输入/输出），并且易于逐步遵循。Strength: Well-structured sections and smooth narrative; concise, readable prose; all specialized terms and assumptions are clearly defined and consistently used; pseudocode/code/workflows are self-contained, well-documented (clear variable names, inputs/outputs), and easy to follow step-by-step.

  4. 弱点：阐述不清楚或杂乱，行话过多或冗余；定义缺失/不明确或术语误用；令人困惑或不完整的伪代码/代码/工作流程（未定义的参数、缺少步骤、不透明的变量名称）。Weakness: Unclear or disorganized exposition, excessive jargon or redundancy; missing/ambiguous definitions or misuse of terms; confusing or incomplete pseudocode/code/workflows (undefined parameters, missing steps, opaque variable names).

  5. CLAR-NOT（符号和数学解释清晰度）：考虑数学符号是否一致、定义和解释是否良好。CLAR-NOT (Notation & Mathematical Explanation Clarity): Considers whether mathematical notation is consistent, well defined and explained.

  6. 优点：突出明确的定义、一致的符号和Strength: Highlights clear definitions, consistent notation and

  7. 很好解释的推导。well-explained derivations.

  8. 弱点：指出不一致的符号、缺少定义或无法解释的数学步骤。Weakness: Notes inconsistent symbols, missing definitions or unexplained mathematical steps.

  9. CLAR-FIG（图形和视觉辅助清晰度）：评估图形是否CLAR-FIG (Figures & Visual Aids Clarity): Evaluates whether figures,

  10. 表格、图表和可视化清晰易读，标签正确，有助于理解。tables, plots and visualisations are legible, properly labelled, and aid understanding.

  11. 优点：赞扬内容丰富、设计精良的人物，清楚地表明Strength: Praises informative and well-designed figures that clearly

  12. 说明结果或架构。illustrate results or architectures.

  13. 弱点：指出难以辨认的情节、缺失的标签、令人困惑的配色方案或误导性的可视化。Weakness: Points out illegible plots, missing labels, confusing colour schemes or misleading visualisations.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审查分类提示（第 3 部分）Prompts for Review Classification (Part 3)

  1. SIGN（意义）：结果对社区有影响吗？其他人（研究人员或实践者）是否可能使用这些想法或在此基础上进行构建？提交的内容是否比以前的工作更好地解决了一项艰巨的任务？它是否以明显的方式增进了我们对该主题的理解/知识？它是否提供了独特的数据、关于现有数据的独特结论，或者独特的理论或实验方法？SIGN (Significance): Are the results impactful for the community? Are others (researchers or practitioners) likely to use the ideas or build on them? Does the submission address a difficult task in a better way than previous work? Does it advance our understanding/knowledge on the topic in a demonstrable way? Does it provide unique data, unique conclusions about existing data, or a unique theoretical or experimental approach?

  2. SIGN-BRD（广泛研究影响）：评估该工作是否解决了广泛重要的问题或以可能影响多个研究领域的方式增进理解。 - 优势：指出该工作解决了重大的、广泛相关的挑战或引入了可能影响许多研究人员的范式。SIGN-BRD (Broad Research Impact): Evaluates whether the work addresses a broadly important problem or advances understanding in a way that is likely to influence multiple research areas. - Strength: Notes that the work tackles a significant, widely relevant challenge or introduces a paradigm that may influence many researchers.

  3. 弱点：表明这项工作解决了一个利基或已经被充分探索的问题，但更广泛的兴趣有限。 - SIGN-DOM（领域/应用影响）：评估特定应用领域（例如 NLP、机器人、医疗保健）贡献的重要性。Weakness: Suggests that the work addresses a niche or already well-explored problem with limited broader interest. - SIGN-DOM (Domain/Applied Impact): Assesses the significance of the contribution for a specific application domain (e.g., NLP, robotics, healthcare).

  4. 优点：强调该方法如何优于现有方法或解决专业领域的重要挑战。Strength: Highlights how the method outperforms existing approaches or addresses an important challenge in a specialised field.

  5. 弱点：指出领域相关性有限、特定领域基准的演示不充分或领域优势不令人信服。Weakness: Notes limited domain relevance, inadequate demonstration on domain-specific benchmarks or unconvincing domain benefits.

  6. SIGN-SOT（对最先进技术的改进）：重点关注相对于当前最先进方法的改进的幅度和重要性。 - 优势：赞扬在适当的统计支持下，在强大的基线基础上取得实质性、一致的绩效提升。SIGN-SOT (Improvement Over State of the Art): Focuses on the magnitude and importance of improvements relative to current state-of-the-art methods. - Strength: Praises substantial, consistent performance gains over strong baselines with appropriate statistical support.

  7. 弱点：批评边际或统计上微不足道的改进，或仅在有利的数据集上进行改进。Weakness: Criticises marginal or statistically insignificant improvements, or improvements only on favourable datasets.

  8. SIGN-IMP（现实世界和社会影响）：考虑工作的潜在实际和社会影响，包括效益、风险、公平性、环境影响和可达性。SIGN-IMP (Real-World & Societal Impact): Considers the potential practical and societal ramifications of the work, including benefits, risks, fairness, environmental impacts and accessibility.

  9. 优势：认可对社会效益、危害、公平、环境影响和可能的缓解措施的深思熟虑的讨论。 - 弱点：指出缺乏对社会影响的考虑或未能讨论谁受益、谁可能受到伤害。Strength: Recognises thoughtful discussion of societal benefits, harms, fairness, environmental impact and possible mitigations. - Weakness: Points out a lack of consideration of societal impact or failure to discuss who benefits and who may be harmed.

  10. ORIG（原创性）：这项工作是否提供了新的见解、加深了理解或强调了现有方法的重要属性？这项工作与之前的贡献有何不同，并提供了相关引文，是否清楚？这项工作是否引入了推动该领域发展的新颖任务或方法？这项工作是否提供了现有技术的新颖组合，并且这种组合背后的推理是否清晰？正如上述问题所表明的，原创性并不一定需要引入全新的方法。相反，通过评估现有方法提供新颖见解或展示提高的效率、公平性等的工作也同样有价值。ORIG (Originality): Does the work provide new insights, deepen understanding, or highlight important properties of existing methods? Is it clear how this work differs from previous contributions, with relevant citations provided? Does the work introduce novel tasks or methods that advance the field? Does this work offer a novel combination of existing techniques, and is the reasoning behind this combination well-articulated? As the questions above indicates, originality does not necessarily require introducing an entirely new method. Rather, a work that provides novel insights by evaluating existing methods, or demonstrates improved efficiency, fairness, etc. is also equally valuable.

  • ORIG-PROB（新问题表述）：对引入ORIG-PROB (Novel Problem Formulation): Comments on the introduction of a

  • 为研究开辟新途径的数据集。datasets that open new avenues for research.

  • 弱点：指出该问题是现有任务的微小变化Weakness: Notes that the problem is a minor variation of existing tasks

  • 无法产生有意义的见解。not yield meaningful insights.

  • 提出新的实验设置、基准、评估协议或收集新的数据集。proposes new experimental setups, benchmarks, evaluation protocols or collects new datasets.

  • 优点：赞扬引入有意义的新数据集、基准或Strength: Praises introduction of meaningful new datasets, benchmarks or

  • 评估协议。evaluation protocols.

  • 弱点：注意到实验设置是衍生的或没有添加Weakness: Notes that the experimental setup is derivative or does not add

  • 论文以原创的方式结合了众所周知的技术，以及这种结合背后的基本原理是否令人信服。the paper combines well-known techniques in an original way and whether the rationale behind the combination is compelling.

  • 提供批判性评估、消除或负面结果，显示现有方法的局限性。that provide critical evaluations, ablations or negative results showing limitations of existing methods.

  • 挑战假设的负面结果。negative results that challenge assumptions.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审查分类提示（第 5 部分）Prompts for Review Classification (Part 5)

  1. POL（政策/合规）：包含与政策或合规相关的问题，例如道德、数据/隐私合规、匿名规则、剽窃、许可和更广泛的影响。这些问题通常需要检查会议或法律政策的遵守情况，并且可能涉及道德考虑。POL (Policy/Compliance): Encompasses policy- or compliance-related concerns such as ethics, data/privacy compliance, anonymity rules, plagiarism, licensing, and broader impact. These issues often require checking adherence to conference or legal policies and may involve ethical considerations.

  2. POL-ETH（道德与负责任的人工智能合规性）：解决道德问题，POL-ETH (Ethics & Responsible AI Compliance): Addresses ethical concerns,

  3. 包括公平、偏见、对边缘群体的伤害、双重用途以及作者是否适当讨论和减轻道德风险。including fairness, bias, harms to marginalised populations, dual use and whether the authors appropriately discuss and mitigate ethical risks.

  4. 优点：注意到对潜在危害、公平性的彻底考虑，Strength: Notes thorough consideration of potential harms, fairness,

  5. 隐私和缓解策略。privacy and mitigation strategies.

  6. 弱点：标记未解决的道德问题、潜在危害或Weakness: Flags unaddressed ethical issues, potential harms or

  7. 对偏见的讨论不够充分。insufficient discussion of biases.

  8. POL-DAT（数据使用和隐私合规性）：考虑数据是否POL-DAT (Data Usage & Privacy Compliance): Considers whether the data

  9. 本文中使用的内容符合隐私法规和许可条款（例如同意、个人身份信息）。used in the paper comply with privacy regulations and licensing terms (e.g., consent, personally identifiable information).

  10. 优点：赞扬遵守数据使用政策、适当的匿名化Strength: Praises adherence to data-use policies, proper anonymisation

  11. 和适当的许可。and appropriate licensing.

  12. 弱点：指出潜在的隐私侵犯、数据不足Weakness: Points out potential privacy violations, insufficient data

  13. 同意或滥用许可数据。consent or misuse of licensed data.

  14. POL-ANO（双盲或匿名违规）：关注是否POL-ANO (Double-Blind or Anonymity Violations): Concerns whether the

  15. 提交内容无意中泄露了作者身份或机构隶属关系，违反了双盲审查政策。submission inadvertently reveals author identities or institutional affiliations, violating double-blind review policies.

  16. 优点：确认论文适当地匿名了作者和Strength: Confirms that the paper appropriately anonymises authors and

  17. 隶属关系。affiliations.

  18. 弱点：突出显示自我识别文本、致谢或 URLWeakness: Highlights self-identifying text, acknowledgements or URLs that

  19. 妥协匿名。compromise anonymity.

  20. POL-PLG（剽窃和双重提交）：解决剽窃问题，POL-PLG (Plagiarism & Dual Submission): Addresses issues of plagiarism,

  21. 自我剽窃，或向多个场所双重提交。self-plagiarism, or dual submission to multiple venues.

  22. 优点：确认内容的原创性、正确的引用以及没有Strength: Confirms originality of content, proper citations and no

  23. 重复提交的证据。evidence of duplicate submission.

  24. 弱点：注释文本或图形重叠、未经认可的材料重复使用Weakness: Notes textual or figure overlap, uncredited reuse of material

  25. 或同时提交。or simultaneous submission.

  26. POL-IMP（更广泛的影响和社会考虑）：评估是否POL-IMP (Broader Impact & Societal Considerations): Evaluates whether the

  27. 作者填写所需的清单并讨论他们的工作对社会的更广泛影响。authors complete required checklists and discuss the broader impact of their work on society.

  28. 优势：全面、诚实、影响更广泛的声明和要求Strength: Comprehensive and honest broader-impact statements and required

  29. 清单；记录在案的 IRB/道德批准（如适用）；明确且正确的软件/数据许可（以及许可条款内的使用）；明确的同意/批准标识符和合规证据。checklists; documented IRB/ethics approvals where applicable; explicit and correct software/data licences (and usage within licence terms); clear consent/approval identifiers and compliance evidence.

  30. 弱点：影响陈述缺失或样板；缺少/不清楚所需的清单；在需要时缺少 IRB 或道德批准；不明确或不兼容的许可；不合规使用的证据（例如，违反数据集或代码许可条款）。Weakness: Missing or boilerplate impact statements; absent/unclear required checklists; missing IRB or ethics approval where needed; ambiguous or incompatible licensing; evidence of non-compliant use (e.g., violating dataset or code licence terms).

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审查分类提示（第 6 部分）Prompts for Review Classification (Part 6)

消歧边界规则Boundary Rules for Disambiguation

  1. CLAR-WRT 与 QUAL-EXP：关于缺失基线或实验不充分的评论属于 QUAL-EXP，即使糟糕的写作使实验难以遵循；仅与可读性有关的问题属于 CLAR-WRT。CLAR-WRT vs. QUAL-EXP: Comments about missing baselines or inadequate experiments belong under QUAL-EXP, even when poor writing makes experiments hard to follow; issues solely about readability go under CLAR-WRT.

  2. CLAR-FIG 与 QUAL-EXP：对难以辨认或不清楚的情节的批评转到 CLAR-FIG；关于缺少绘图或缺少基线曲线的批评请提交至 QUAL-EXP。CLAR-FIG vs. QUAL-EXP: Critiques about illegible or unclear plots go to CLAR-FIG; critiques about missing plots or missing baseline curves go to QUAL-EXP.

  3. QUAL-REP 与 CLAR-NOT：缺失代码、超参数或数据分割属于 QUAL-REP；未定义的变量或不一致的符号属于 CLAR-NOT。QUAL-REP vs. CLAR-NOT: Missing code, hyperparameters or data splits fall under QUAL-REP; undefined variables or inconsistent notation fall under CLAR-NOT.

  4. SIGN-IMP 与 POL-IMP：在没有强制性检查表的情况下对社会效益或危害的一般讨论由 SIGN-IMP 进行；有关遵守所需影响声明的意见将提交至 POL-IMP。SIGN-IMP vs. POL-IMP: General discussion of societal benefits or harms without mandatory checklists goes to SIGN-IMP; comments about compliance with required impact statements go to POL-IMP.

  5. ORIG-EXP vs. QUAL-EXP：引入新的数据集或基准是 ORIG-EXP；批评现有数据集实验的彻底性是 QUAL-EXP。ORIG-EXP vs. QUAL-EXP: Introducing a new dataset or benchmark is ORIG-EXP; critiquing the thoroughness of experiments on existing datasets is QUAL-EXP.

  6. POL-ETH 与 SIGN-IMP：对边缘群体的道德违规、偏见或伤害属于 POL-ETH；没有合规问题的高级别影响评论属于 SIGN-IMP。POL-ETH vs. SIGN-IMP: Ethical violations, bias or harm to marginalised groups belong in POL-ETH; high-level impact comments without compliance issues belong in SIGN-IMP.

  7. QUAL-CMP 与 SIGN-SOT：缺少基线或不完整的文献综述是 QUAL-CMP；判断报告的改进是否有意义由 SIGN-SOT 进行。QUAL-CMP vs. SIGN-SOT: Missing baselines or incomplete literature reviews are QUAL-CMP; judging whether reported improvements are meaningful goes to SIGN-SOT.

  8. ORIG-COM vs. ORIG-MTH：以独创的方式结合现有方法是ORIG-COM；提出全新的算法是 ORIG-MTH。ORIG-COM vs. ORIG-MTH: Combining existing methods in an original way is ORIG-COM; proposing entirely new algorithms is ORIG-MTH.

  9. QUAL-CMP 与 QUAL-EXP：缺失/不相关或过时的基线和文献映射QUAL-CMP vs. QUAL-EXP: Missing/irrelevant or outdated baselines and literature mapping→质量-CMP；评估协议设计（指标、分割、调整公平性、测试覆盖率）QUAL-CMP; evaluation protocol design (metrics, splits, tuning fairness, test coverage)→合格-实验。QUAL-EXP.

  10. ORIG-MTH 与 QUAL-EXP：算法/架构是否真正新颖与细微调整ORIG-MTH vs. QUAL-EXP: Whether the algorithm/architecture is truly novel vs. a minor tweak→原始-MTH；实验是否充分验证了（新颖与否）方法ORIG-MTH; whether experiments sufficiently validate the (novel or not) method→合格-实验。QUAL-EXP.

[输出：][Output:][返回][Return][仅][only][the][applicable][label(s).] 如果审阅者提到多个不同的评论，请分配不同的标签，并用逗号分隔。如果审稿人只提到一条评论，则分配最合理的单个标签。不要在输出中包含任何其他文字或解释。[the][applicable][label(s).] If the reviewer mentions multiple distinct comments, assign different labels, separated by commas. If the reviewer mentions only one comment, assign the most reasonable single label. Do not include any other words or explanation in the output.

审阅者间冲突识别的提示Prompts for Inter-Reviewer Conflict Identification

您是一名专业的学术同行评审分析助理。仅关注检测每个讨论点内审稿人意见之间的冲突。You are a professional academic peer review analysis assistant. Focus ONLY on detecting conflicts between reviewer opinions within each discussion point.

准确判断观点是否真正矛盾或互补。Be precise about whether viewpoints are truly contradictory vs. complementary.

请分析每个讨论点中审稿人的意见，以确定真正的冲突。Please analyze reviewer opinions within each discussion point to identify GENUINE conflicts.

冲突检测规则： 1. **[定义][冲突：] ** - 在同一方面直接矛盾（例如，审稿人 A 说“方法新颖”，审稿人 B 说“方法缺乏新颖性”）。 - 关于同一特定功能的相反情绪（积极与消极）。Rules for Conflict Detection: 1. **[Definition][of][Conflict:] ** - Direct contradiction on the IDENTICAL aspect (e.g., Reviewer A says ’method is novel’, Reviewer B says ’method lacks novelty’). - Opposing sentiments (Positive vs. Negative) regarding the same specific feature.

  1. [什么][是][不是][a][冲突][（至关重要）：] ** -[What][is][NOT][a][Conflict][(Crucial):] ** -粒度差异：** 具体示例（例如“适用于大图像”）与一般陈述（例如“概括得很好”）是互补的，而不是冲突的。Granularity Differences:** Specific examples (e.g., ’works on large images’) vs. General statements (e.g., ’generalizes well’) are COMPLEMENTARY, not conflicting.

  2. 不同方面：Different Aspects:对不同维度（例如“效率”与“准确性”）的看法并不冲突。 -Opinions on different dimensions (e.g., ’efficiency’ vs ’accuracy’) are not conflicts. -非论证性文本：Non-Argumentative Text:忽略涉及纯粹事实列表、引文、参考文献或格式工件（例如“NO”、块 ID）的冲突。Ignore conflicts involving purely factual lists, citations, references, or formatting artifacts (e.g., ’NO.’, block IDs).

  3. 同一审稿人：Same Reviewer:来自同一审稿人姓名的意见通常会完善或列出自己观点的详细信息，不应被标记为冲突。Opinions from the same reviewer name typically refine or list details for their own points and should not be flagged as conflicts.

任务：Task:

对于每个讨论点 ID，检查所有相关的审阅者意见。第 1 步：过滤掉非意见文本（引文、元数据）。步骤2：检查意见是否对同一核心问题表达相反的观点。步骤3：输出冲突状态。For each discussion point ID, examine all associated reviewer opinions. Step 1: Filter out non-opinion text (citations, meta-data). Step 2: Check if opinions express opposing views on the same core issue. Step 3: Output conflict status.

输出格式 (JSON)： - point ~~c~~ onflicts: point ~~i~~ d: "has ~~c~~ onflict": bool 讨论点组：Output Format (JSON): - point ~~c~~ onflicts: point ~~i~~ d: "has ~~c~~ onflict": bool Discussion Point Groups:{提示~~p~~艺术prompt ~~p~~ arts}

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

审阅者与作者冲突识别的提示Prompts for Reviewer-Author Conflict Identification

您是一名专业的学术同行评审分析助理。重点关注作者是否明确反驳审稿人的意见。仔细分析，但只输出请求的 JSON 数组。You are a professional academic peer review analysis assistant. Focus on detecting if authors explicitly refute reviewer opinions. Analyze carefully but only output the JSON array as requested.

您正在分析同行评审对话。 f“审稿人”You are analyzing a peer review conversation. f"Reviewer ’{审稿人~~n~~ amereviewer ~~n~~ ame}’有’ has{伦（意见）len(opinions)}本文中的意见。”你的任务是确定作者的回应是否明确反驳了每个观点。opinions in this paper." Your task is to determine if the author’s responses explicitly refute each opinion.

“反驳”的关键指南： 1.CRITICAL GUIDELINES FOR ’REFUTATION’: 1.[什么][是][a][反驳？] ** - 作者明确认为审稿人的评论是[What][is][a][Refutation?] ** - The author explicitly argues that the reviewer’s comment is事实上不正确factually incorrect, 不相关的irrelevant，或基于, or based on a误解**。 - 信号：“我们不同意”、“审稿人不正确”、“这是一个误解”、“我们认为没有必要”。misunderstanding**. - Signals: ’We disagree’, ’The reviewer is incorrect’, ’This is a misunderstanding’, ’We do not think this is necessary’.

  1. [什么][是][不是][a][反驳][（错误][肯定][要][避免）：] ** -[What][is][NOT][a][Refutation][(False][Positives][to][AVOID):] ** -接受+差异化：Acceptance + Differentiation:如果审稿人要求与基线（例如 ConViT [6]）进行比较，并且作者添加了比较但解释了If the reviewer asks to compare with a baseline (e.g., ConViT [6]), and the author adds the comparison but explains[为什么][why][他们的][方法][是][不同/更好][（例如，]['不像][ConViT，][我们][做][X...'），]这是[their][method][is][different/better][(e.g.,][’Unlike][ConViT,][we][do][X...’),] this is合规性COMPLIANCE，不反驳。 -, not refutation. -技术说明：Technical Clarification:用于解释技术边界与基线的诸如“但是，我们的方法......”之类的短语并不是审稿人的反驳。 -Phrases like ’However, our method...’ used to explain technical boundaries vs. baselines are NOT refutations of the reviewer. -引文/元数据：Citation/Metadata:如果“意见”文本只是一个参考字符串（例如，“- [6] d'Ascoli...”），那么它是数据，而不是参数。这是无法反驳的。If the ’opinion’ text is merely a reference string (e.g., ’- [6] d’Ascoli...’), it is data, not an argument. It CANNOT be refuted.[总是][返回][错误][针对][引用。] **[Always][return][False][for][citations.] **

审查分割模型的提示Prompts for ReviewSplit Model

您将获得同行评审讨论，分为You are given a peer-review discussion split into{编号num}零件，每个零件都带有“零件”前缀parts, each prefixed with "Part{{ id }}：”。每个部分都是讨论中的一条评论。:". Each part is one comment in the discussion.

目标GOAL

为每个部分分配数字标签，以便有关同一讨论点的所有部分共享相同的标签。Assign numeric labels to EVERY part so that all parts about the same discussion Point share the same label.

定义和规则：Definitions and rules:

  • 一个点是审稿人提出的（或在回复中讨论的）一个基本的优点、缺点或问题。A Point is one atomic strength, weakness, or question raised by a reviewer (or discussed in replies).

  • 标签是从 1 开始的整数（“标签 1”、“标签 2”...）。Labels are integers starting from 1 ("Label 1", "Label 2", ...).

  • 如果审阅者稍后在讨论中提出新的要点，请为这些要点分配新标签。尽量避免对讨论中相距很远的点使用相同的标签，即使它们是相关的。 - 对于既不是优点也不是缺点的仅摘要部分，将整个摘要视为一个点，并为整个摘要分配一个标签。If a reviewer raises new Points later in the discussion, assign new labels to those Points. Try to avoid using the same label for Points that are very far apart in the discussion, even if they are related. - For summary-only parts that are neither strengths nor weaknesses, treat the entire summary as one single Point and assign one label to the whole summary.

输出格式OUTPUT FORMAT

精确生产Produce exactly{编号num}行，每个部分一行，按输入顺序排列：第 k 部分：标签 Xlines, one per part, in input order: Part k: Label X

{分段审稿意见Segmented reviewer comments}

查看分类模型的提示Prompts for ReviewClassify Model

您会收到审稿人的评论（以及可选的作者的回复）。您的任务是使用以下分类法对评论进行分类。输出应仅包含子类别名称。 # 分类学 ... ... ### 消歧边界规则 1. CLAR-WRT 与 QUAL-EXP：有关缺失基线或不充分实验的评论属于 QUAL-EXP，即使糟糕的写作使实验难以遵循；仅与可读性有关的问题属于 CLAR-WRT。 2. CLAR-FIG vs. QUAL-EXP：对难以辨认或不清楚的情节的批评请转到 CLAR-FIG；关于缺少绘图或缺少基线曲线的批评请提交至 QUAL-EXP。You are given a reviewer comment (and optionally the authors’ response). Your task is to classify the comment using the following taxonomy. The output should only contain the sub-category name. # Taxonomy ... ... ### Boundary Rules for Disambiguation 1. CLAR-WRT vs. QUAL-EXP: Comments about missing baselines or inadequate experiments belong under QUAL-EXP, even when poor writing makes experiments hard to follow; issues solely about readability go under CLAR-WRT. 2. CLAR-FIG vs. QUAL-EXP: Critiques about illegible or unclear plots go to CLAR-FIG; critiques about missing plots or missing baseline curves go to QUAL-EXP.

  1. QUAL-REP 与 CLAR-NOT：缺失代码、超参数或数据分割属于 QUAL-REP；未定义的变量或不一致的符号属于 CLAR-NOT。QUAL-REP vs. CLAR-NOT: Missing code, hyperparameters or data splits fall under QUAL-REP; undefined variables or inconsistent notation fall under CLAR-NOT.

  2. SIGN-IMP 与 POL-IMP：在没有强制性检查表的情况下对社会效益或危害的一般讨论由 SIGN-IMP 进行；有关遵守所需影响声明的意见将提交至 POL-IMP。SIGN-IMP vs. POL-IMP: General discussion of societal benefits or harms without mandatory checklists goes to SIGN-IMP; comments about compliance with required impact statements go to POL-IMP.

  3. ORIG-EXP vs. QUAL-EXP：引入新的数据集或基准是 ORIG-EXP；批评现有数据集实验的彻底性是 QUAL-EXP。ORIG-EXP vs. QUAL-EXP: Introducing a new dataset or benchmark is ORIG-EXP; critiquing the thoroughness of experiments on existing datasets is QUAL-EXP.

  4. POL-ETH 与 SIGN-IMP：对边缘群体的道德违规、偏见或伤害属于 POL-ETH；没有合规问题的高级别影响评论属于 SIGN-IMP。POL-ETH vs. SIGN-IMP: Ethical violations, bias or harm to marginalised groups belong in POL-ETH; high-level impact comments without compliance issues belong in SIGN-IMP.

  5. QUAL-CMP 与 SIGN-SOT：缺少基线或不完整的文献综述是 QUAL-CMP；判断报告的改进是否有意义由 SIGN-SOT 进行。QUAL-CMP vs. SIGN-SOT: Missing baselines or incomplete literature reviews are QUAL-CMP; judging whether reported improvements are meaningful goes to SIGN-SOT.

  6. ORIG-COM vs. ORIG-MTH：以独创的方式结合现有方法是ORIG-COM；提出全新的算法是 ORIG-MTH。ORIG-COM vs. ORIG-MTH: Combining existing methods in an original way is ORIG-COM; proposing entirely new algorithms is ORIG-MTH.

  7. QUAL-CMP 与 QUAL-EXP：缺失/不相关或过时的基线和文献映射QUAL-CMP vs. QUAL-EXP: Missing/irrelevant or outdated baselines and literature mapping→质量-CMP；评估协议设计（指标、分割、调整公平性、测试覆盖率）QUAL-CMP; evaluation protocol design (metrics, splits, tuning fairness, test coverage)→合格-实验。QUAL-EXP.

  8. ORIG-MTH 与 QUAL-EXP：算法/架构是否真正新颖与细微调整ORIG-MTH vs. QUAL-EXP: Whether the algorithm/architecture is truly novel vs. a minor tweak→原始-MTH；实验是否充分验证了（新颖与否）方法ORIG-MTH; whether experiments sufficiently validate the (novel or not) method→合格-实验。QUAL-EXP.

[输出：][Output:][返回][Return][仅][only][the][applicable][label(s).] 如果审阅者提到多个不同的评论，请分配不同的标签，并用逗号分隔。如果审稿人只提到一条评论，则分配最合理的单个标签。不要在输出中包含任何其他文字或解释。[the][applicable][label(s).] If the reviewer mentions multiple distinct comments, assign different labels, separated by commas. If the reviewer mentions only one comment, assign the most reasonable single label. Do not include any other words or explanation in the output.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

E.2。 CoCoreviewBench 评估提示E.2. Prompts for CoCoreviewBench Evaluation

我们在下表中列出了论文第 5 节中使用的所有提示，包括：We present all the prompts used in our paper in §5 in the following tables, including:

  • 以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoReviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metrics of CoCoReviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge：我们输入人工智能评论以及相应的人类参考资料。此提示用于通过与人类参考进行比较来评估输入人工智能审核的质量。提示设计很大程度上遵循 Garg 等人的观点。 (2025) 和 Sadallah 等人。 （2025）。此外，正如正文中提到的，正确性和彻底性是通过与人类参考文献进行比较来评分的，以减少法学硕士作为法官的偏见。为了确保法官不依赖自己的知识，而是严格与人类进行比较，我们做了一些措辞上的改变：我们将正确性重命名为对齐，因此法学硕士侧重于与人类的一致性，并将彻底性重命名为完整性，因此法学硕士侧重于相对于人类的广度和深度，从而实现更稳健的评估。: We input an AI review together with the corresponding human references. This prompt is used to evaluate the quality of the input AI review by comparing it against the human references. The prompt design largely follows Garg et al. (2025) and Sadallah et al. (2025). In addition, as mentioned in the main text, Correctness and Thoroughness are scored by comparison with human references to reduce LLM-as-a-Judge bias. To ensure the judge does not rely on its own knowledge but instead strictly compares with humans, we make slight wording changes: we rename Correctness to Alignment, so the LLM focuses on consistency with humans, and rename Thoroughness to Completeness, so the LLM focuses on breadth and depth relative to humans, enabling a more robust evaluation.

  • 通用 AI 审稿人的提示Prompts for General AI Reviewer：对于专有的人工智能审稿人，我们直接使用他们的论文/代码中指定的提示。对于一般模型，我们遵循 Lu 等人的方法。 (2024) 并使用他们的提示来生成最终评论。: For proprietary AI reviewers, we directly use the prompts specified in their papers/code. For general models, we follow Lu et al. (2024) and use their prompt to generate the final review.

  • 提示我们训练有素的 AI 审阅代理Prompts for Our Trained AI Reviewer Agent：对于我们训练有素的人工智能审阅代理，我们设计的提示尽可能简单，仅更改输入分类法以在不同类别的审阅代理之间切换。: For our trained AI reviewer agent, we design prompts to be as simple as possible, changing only the input taxonomy to switch among review agents for different categories.

  3. [具体][vs.]模糊[Concrete][vs.] Vague:

  5. 模糊：Vague:在确定该操作后，作者仍然不知道如何执行该操作。After identifying the action, the authors still don’t know how to carry out this action.

[重要性：][Importance:][它][更多][重要][针对][行动][目标][成为][具体][因此][让]作者知道如何应用它们。直接陈述而不是推断的行动也是首选。[It’s][more][important][for][actions][to][be][concrete][so][that] authors know how to apply them. It’s also preferred for actions to be stated directly rather than inferred.

以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoreviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示（第 4 部分）Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metric of CoCoreviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge (Part 4)

  1. [接地：][Grounding:][如何][好][作者][能够][识别]所讨论的论文[的][具体][部分]。 -[How][well][the][authors][can][identify][the][specific][part][of] the paper being addressed. -弱接地：Weak Grounding:作者可以做出有根据的猜测，但无法准确识别引用的部分。The author can make an educated guess but cannot precisely identify the referenced part.

  2. 全面接地：Full Grounding:作者可以准确地指出所讨论的部分、表格、图形或独特方面。这可以通过以下方式实现： - 文字提及章节、表格、图形等。The author can accurately pinpoint the section, table, figure, or unique aspect being addressed. This can be achieved through: - Literal mentions of sections, tables, figures, etc.

  3. 提及论文的独特元素。 - 明确暗示相关部分但未明确命名的一般注释。Mentions of unique elements of the paper. - General comments that clearly imply the relevant parts without explicitly naming them.

  4. [具体：][Specificity:][如何][清楚地][][评论][详细信息][什么][是][错误][或]在引用的部分中缺失。如果提到外部工作，它还会评估是否提供了具体示例。[How][clearly][the][comment][details][what][is][wrong][or] missing in the referenced part. If external work is mentioned, it also evaluates whether specific examples are provided.

[重要性：][Importance:][这][更][重要][对于][评论][要][要][接地气][比][要]具体。[It’s][more][important][for][the][comment][to][be][grounded][than][to] be specific.

**[基础][特异性][尺度][(1-5):] ****[Grounding][Specificity][Scale][(1-5):] **

  1. [不][接地] ** -[Not][Grounded] ** -定义**：这个评论根本没有根据。它没有标识论文中的特定区域。该评论非常不具体。Definition**: This comment is not grounded at all. It does not identify a specific area in the paper. The comment is highly unspecific.

  2. [弱][接地][和][不][特定] ** -[Weakly][Grounded][and][Not][Specific] ** -定义**：作者无法自信地确定评论针对的是哪一部分。此外，该评论没有具体说明这部分需要解决什么问题。Definition**: The authors cannot confidently determine which part the comment addresses. Further, the comment does not specify what needs to be addressed in this part.

  3. [弱][接地][和][特定] ** -[Weakly][Grounded][and][Specific] ** -定义**：作者无法自信地确定评论针对的是哪一部分。然而，评论明确指出了这部分需要解决的问题。Definition**: The authors cannot confidently determine which part the comment addresses. However, the comment clearly specifies what needs to be addressed in this part.

  4. [完全][接地][和][低于特定] ** -[Fully][Grounded][and][Under-Specific] ** -定义**：评论明确提到了它涉及论文的哪一部分，或者对作者来说应该是显而易见的。然而，这个评论并没有具体说明这部分需要解决什么问题。Definition**: The comment explicitly mentions which part of the paper it addresses, or it should be obvious to the authors. However, this comment does not specify what needs to be addressed in this part.

  5. [完全][接地][和][特定] ** -[Fully][Grounded][and][Specific] ** -定义**：评论明确提到了它涉及论文的哪一部分，这对作者来说是显而易见的。注释指定了这部分需要解决的问题。Definition**: The comment explicitly mentions which part of the paper it addresses, and it is obvious to the authors. The comment specifies what needs to be addressed in this part.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoreviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示（第 5 部分）Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metric of CoCoreviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge (Part 5)

方面：可验证性**[可验证性]**Aspect: verifiability **[Verifiability] **

[定义：][Definition:][评估][如何][很好][一项][主张][通过][检查][所提供的]推理、常识或外部参考资料得到[验证]。目的是确保审稿意见有助于作者改进他们的工作。[Assess][how][well][a][claim][is][verified][by][examining][the] reasoning, common knowledge, or external references provided. The purpose is to ensure that the review comment helps the authors improve their work.

**[验证][方法：] ** 如果得到以下一项或多项支持，则声明被视为可验证：**[Verification][Methods:] ** A claim is considered verifiable if supported by one or more of the following:

  • 逻辑推理Logical reasoning{ 清楚解释该主张为何有效。{ A clear explanation of why the claim is valid.

    • 常识Common knowledge{ 参考公认的实践或标准。 -{ Reference to well-accepted practices or standards. -外部参考External references{ 引用相关文献、数据或来源。{ Citation of relevant literature, data, or sources.

[可验证性][规模][(1{5][&][X):] ** 1。[Verifiability][Scale][(1{5][&][X):] ** 1.[1:] 无法验证[1:] Unverifiable- 该评论包含没有任何支持证据或理由的主张。 2.- The comment contains a claim without any supporting evidence or justification. 2.[2:] 边界可验证[2:] Borderline Verifiable- 提供了一些支持，但模糊、不充分或难以遵循。 3.- Some support is provided, but it is vague, insufficient, or difficult to follow. 3.[3:] 有点可验证[3:] Somewhat Verifiable- 该主张有一定的理由，但缺乏关键要素（例如示例、参考文献）。 4.- The claim has some justification but lacks key elements (e.g., examples, references). 4.[4:] 大部分可验证[4:] Mostly Verifiable- 该主张得到充分支持，但在解释或参考文献中存在细微差距。 5.- The claim is well-supported but has minor gaps in explanation or references. 5.[5:] 完全可验证** - 该主张得到明确、充分和有力的证据的充分支持，例如：[5:] Fully Verifiable** - The claim is thoroughly supported by explicit, sufficient, and robust evidence, such as:

  • 清晰的推理和精确的解释。 - 对外部作品的具体参考。Clear reasoning and precise explanations. - Specific references to external works.

  • 逻辑性强、无懈可击的常识性论证。Logical and unassailable common-sense arguments.

  • X. X：无索赔X: No Claim

  • 评论仅包含事实性的描述性陈述，不包含主张、意见或建议。The comment contains only factual, descriptive statements without claims, opinions, or suggestions.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoreviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示（第 6 部分）Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metric of CoCoreviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge (Part 6)

方面：对齐 **[对齐] **Aspect: alignment **[Alignment] **

[定义：][Definition:][措施][如何][好][a][候选人][审查][评论][与]黄金参考意见在以下方面[Measures][how][well][a][candidate][review][comment][aligns][with] the gold reference opinions in terms of定向姿态directional stance（同意与不同意）和(agreement vs disagreement) and是否表达相同的基本观点whether it expresses the same underlying opinion。一致性并不是指措辞相似，而是指措辞相似。这是关于候选人是否支持与黄金参考相同的想法/批评/赞扬。. Alignment is not about wording similarity; it is about whether the candidate supports the same idea/critique/praise as the gold reference.

**[对齐方式][比例][(1{5):] ****[Alignment][Scale][(1{5):] **

  1. [1:] 反对意见[1:] Opposing Opinion

  2. 定义：Definition:候选人对关键黄金观点表达反对立场（即与主要观点相矛盾），或就同一问题提出相反的结论。The candidate expresses an opposing stance to the key gold opinion(s) (i.e., contradicts the main thrust), or argues the opposite conclusion on the same issue.

  3. [2:] 大部分未对齐[2:] Mostly Misaligned - 定义：Definition:这位候选人在很大程度上是错位的：它要么与黄金的一些关键观点相矛盾，要么以与黄金相冲突的方式来阐述问题。The candidate is largely misaligned: it either contradicts some key gold opinion(s) or frames the issue in a way that conflicts with the gold.

  4. [3:] 混合或不相关[3:] Mixed or Unrelated - 定义：Definition:候选人包括一致和反对的意见，The candidate includes a mixture of aligned and opposing opinions,or它在很大程度上是不相关/正交的（既没有明显对齐也没有明显对立）。它并不一贯支持黄金的立场。it is largely unrelated/orthogonal (neither clearly aligned nor clearly opposing). It does not consistently support the gold’s stance.

  5. [4:] 基本一致[4:] Mostly Aligned - 定义：Definition:候选人大多与黄金的立场和观点一致，但可能会遗漏一些限制/细节、软化/转移重点，或包含不会推翻主要协议的微小偏差。The candidate is mostly aligned with the gold’s stance and opinions, but may miss some constraints/details, soften/shift emphasis, or contain minor deviations that do not overturn the main agreement.

  6. [5:] 完全对齐[5:] Fully Aligned - 定义：Definition:候选人表达了与黄金参考相同的基本观点：它在方向和实质上都符合主要关注/赞扬，没有重大矛盾。The candidate expresses the same underlying opinion(s) as the gold reference: it matches the main concerns/praises in both direction and substance, without material contradictions.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoreviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示（第 7 部分）Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metric of CoCoreviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge (Part 7)

方面：完整性Aspect: completeness

**[完整性] ****[Completeness] **

[定义：][Definition:][措施][如何][完全][a][候选][审查][评论][涵盖]黄金参考意见（人类审稿人评论）。完整性大约是[Measures][how][completely][a][candidate][review][comment][covers] the gold reference opinions (human reviewer comments). Completeness is about覆盖广度coverage breadth在一系列黄金观点中：候选人有意义地解决了多少种不同的黄金担忧/赞扬。across the set of gold opinions: how many distinct gold concerns/praises are meaningfully addressed by the candidate.

完整性有两个组成部分：Completeness has two components:

  1. **[覆盖范围][广度：]****[Coverage][Breadth:] **

  2. 候选人是否提出了少数或许多关键的黄金观点。Whether the candidate addresses few vs many of the key gold opinions.

  3. **[覆盖范围][充分性：] ** - 候选人是否有意义地阐述了所涵盖的黄金观点（不仅仅是模糊的提及）。**[Coverage][Adequacy:] ** - Whether the candidate meaningfully addresses the covered gold opinions (not just a vague mention).

  4. 如果候选人比黄金意见更通用，则该黄金意见应被视为If the candidate is much more generic than a gold opinion, that gold opinion should be treated as未涵盖not covered或者只是弱覆盖。or only weakly covered.

[重要性：][Importance:][广度][是][更多][重要][比][长度。]如果遗漏了关键的黄金观点，长篇候选人仍然可能是不完整的。[Breadth][is][more][important][than][length.] A long candidate can still be incomplete if it misses key gold opinions.

**[完整性][规模][(1{5):] ****[Completeness][Scale][(1{5):] **

  1. [1:] 基本完成[1:] Minimally Complete - 定义：Definition:该候选人没有涵盖或几乎没有涵盖任何关键的黄金观点。大多数黄金意见均未涵盖；任何重叠都是偶然的或极其微弱的。The candidate covers none or almost none of the key gold opinions. Most gold opinions are not covered; any overlap is accidental or extremely weak.

  2. [2:] 低完整性[2:] Low Completeness - 定义：Definition:候选人只涵盖了黄金意见的一小部分，和/或涵盖较弱（一般性提及，但没有解决基本要求）。缺少几个关键的黄金观点。The candidate covers only a small portion of gold opinions, and/or covers them weakly (generic mentions without addressing the underlying request). Several key gold opinions are missing.

  3. [3:] 中等完整性[3:] Moderate Completeness - 定义：Definition:这位候选人提出了一些黄金观点，但忽略了重要的观点。覆盖范围可能参差不齐：一些观点被覆盖，另一些则只被薄弱地覆盖。The candidate covers some gold opinions, but misses important ones. Coverage may be uneven: some opinions covered, others only weakly covered.

  4. [4:] 高完整性[4:] High Completeness - 定义：Definition:候选人涵盖了大多数关键的黄金观点，并且内容总体充足。只有少数不太核心的黄金观点被遗漏或覆盖较弱。The candidate covers most key gold opinions, with generally adequate substance. Only a small number of less-central gold opinions are missing or weakly covered.

  5. [5:] 完全完成[5:] Fully Complete - 定义：Definition:候选人几乎涵盖了所有关键的黄金观点，而且覆盖范围很有意义（不仅仅是话题性的）。很少或根本没有黄金意见未被涵盖。The candidate covers nearly all key gold opinions, and coverage is meaningful (not merely topical). Few or no gold opinions remain not covered.

CoCoReviewBench：面向人工智能审阅者的完整性和正确性基准CoCoReviewBench: A Completeness- and Correctness-Oriented Benchmark for AI Reviewers

以 LLM 为评委的 CoCoreviewBench 的论文级和类别级指标提示（第 8 部分）Prompts for Paper-Level and Category-Level Metric of CoCoreviewBench with LLM-as-a-Judge (Part 8)

方面：净度**[净度]**Aspect: clarity **[Clarity] **

[定义：][Definition:][法官][如何][连贯、][可读、][并且][结构良好][]评论是。清晰度侧重于表达：逻辑流畅、易于阅读以及语言是否简洁而不是令人困惑或冗长。[Judges][how][coherent,][readable,][and][well-structured][the] comment is. Clarity focuses on delivery: logical flow, ease of reading, and whether the language is concise rather than confusing or verbose.

**[清晰度][比例][(1{5):] ****[Clarity][Scale][(1{5):] **

  1. [1:] 不清楚/难以阅读[1:] Unclear / Hard to Read - 定义：Definition:由于连贯性差（杂乱无章、支离破碎或自相矛盾）、冗长而缺乏信号或措辞不明确，评论令人困惑或难以理解。读者无法轻易识别要点。The comment is confusing or difficult to follow due to poor coherence (disorganized, fragmented, or contradictory), heavy verbosity with little signal, or unclear phrasing. Readers cannot easily identify the main point.

  2. [2:] 大多不清楚[2:] Mostly Unclear - 定义：Definition:该评论存在严重的可读性问题（冗长、句子混乱、结构薄弱）。要点已经存在，但需要费力才能提取出来，而且表达方式模糊了含义。The comment has substantial readability issues (wordy, tangled sentences, weak structure). The main point is present but requires effort to extract, and the delivery obscures meaning.

  3. [3:] 中等清晰[3:] Moderately Clear - 定义：Definition:评论总体上是可读的，要点也可以理解，但表达并不完美（有些冗长、结构不均匀、连贯性较小）。这是可以理解的，但不清晰。The comment is generally readable and the main point can be understood, but delivery is imperfect (some verbosity, uneven structure, minor coherence gaps). It is understandable but not crisp.

  4. [4:] 清除[4:] Clear - 定义：Definition:该评论连贯且易于阅读，并且呈现结构良好。可能存在轻微的冗长或小的结构问题，但它们不会妨碍理解。The comment is coherent and easy to read, with a mostly well-structured presentation. Minor verbosity or small structural issues may exist, but they do not hinder comprehension.

  5. [5:] 非常清晰/简洁[5:] Very Clear / Concise - 定义：Definition:评论简洁、结构合理、可读性强。想法逻辑流畅，措辞高效，要点（以及任何要求）立即可以理解。The comment is concise, well-structured, and highly readable. Ideas flow logically, wording is efficient, and the main point (and any request) is immediately understandable.

在评分时更加客观和保守。严格以JSON方式响应，不提供任何其他内容：Be more objective and conservative in your grading. Respond strictly as JSON, do not provide any other content:{“索赔提取”：“索赔”或“正常”，“可诉性”："claim extraction": "claim" or "normal", "actionability":或“X”，“接地特异性”：or "X", "grounding specificity":，“可验证性”：, "verifiability":或“X”、“对齐”：or "X", "alignment":，“完整性”：, "completeness":，“清晰度”：, "clarity": }
